# Leads

Every lead on quantumai.google and its linked PDF, with its status and the MCP tool that serves it. The `leads` tool returns the same registry.

| Lead | Status | Tool | Statement | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `perspective-factorer` | served | algorithm | The QPU as the factorer: Shor on the exact simulator for every N within its bound, and the bound itself the honest answer beyond it. |  |
| `perspective-reflection` | served | match | The QPU as the mirror: any statement reflected into its hidden factors, its angles against the bounds and its mirror queries, each a lead. |  |
| `perspective-address` | served | verify | The QPU as the address: one UUID that proves its seal, its content and its neighbours cheaply, tampering always slower than the original. |  |
| `perspective-clay` | served | clay | The QPU as the register: the Clay relations recomputed in exact integers, the σ-involution the universal core, in 33 bits. |  |
| `perspective-language` | served | translate | The QPU as the language machine: any language and dialect by vocabulary, offline and token-free, both ways, and prose programmed from a tool's answer. |  |
| `perspective-imagination` | served | imagine | The QPU as the imagination: every lead reflected, every schema door and application family a lead, and more the mind can imagine recorded as leads. |  |
| `perspective-discovery` | served | discover | The QPU as the discoverer: every host's contract read first, every door chosen by what it takes and answers. |  |
| `perspective-judge` | served | readiness | The QPU as the judge of itself: every lead served, refuted or in trial by measurement, and the unit told so. |  |
| `perspective-database` | partial | mongo | The QPU as the database: MongoDB's language over content-addressed entries, history kept by links, any document framework on the RAID. | the language is ported; the adapter on the deployed docket is owed (payload-db-adapter-docket) |
| `perspective-harmonic` | partial | audit | The QPU as the harmonic: a432 the key of every colour, the block's accent from the UUID's middle as CMYK, on every result. | the theme rides on every block and the audit checks the block; a door that answers the css alone is the unit's get_css, not yet served here |
| `perspective-tenants` | partial | audit | The QPU as the host of tenants: one Worker, a docket per host, roles by token, a security collision answered as a procedure. | deployed and measured live on 2026-10-25 (three hosts, roles, collision as a procedure); the roles move to a Payload MCP plugin over MCP (rbac-payload-mcp) and the tenant secrets stay as the fallback |
| `perspective-court` | partial | readiness | The QPU before the court: the unit's honesty gate judges readiness, signs commits by sealed theorems and refuses what it cannot decide. | readiness and signing are wired; qpu's own proofs are not citable at the border (gap-theorems-unsealed) |
| `qpu-balancer-perspective` | served | audit | In one perspective the QPU is the balancer of CPU, GPU and memory across the motherboard and beyond: a heavy computation is done once, addressed by its content, and every later call is served from memory — the computation inverted, the hardware cooled, the battery saved. | measured on 2026-10-25 by the audit, which times every check: the handler serves every repeated tools/call from its balancer by block address within a process; across processes and hosts the docket is that memory (publish writes the artefacts), the next step being tool results themselves as docket entries read before computing |
| `raid-virtualizes-os` | open | — | Quantum apps on the RAID virtualise every operating system and whatever else the mind imagines: an OS image, its packages and its shell are entries and doors like any other, so an app runs from the docket on any host that mounts the toolboxes; development is traced only through leads. | inspiration recorded on 2026-10-25; the unit already serves os, port, alpine and shell doors (found by discovery, read by capability), so the first measurement is a qpu app that reads an Alpine package list through those doors and stores it as docket entries; nothing of this is assumed, each step is a lead until served |
| `payload-port-cloudflare` | open | — | Payload CMS and its dependencies ported whole to Cloudflare Workers under nodejs_compat, its database adapter writing docket entries so that content, media and configuration live on the qpu RAID; every Payload collection and plugin then a toolbox of this surface and every other framework merged the same way, one combinatorial framework hosted on the RAID. | inspiration recorded on 2026-10-25; the two sources are fetched by the wave each run so the templates directory and the Workers Node.js compatibility page are measured, not assumed; the port itself is the author's act on a deployed Worker and docket |
| `payload-db-adapter-docket` | partial | mongo | Payload's mongodb database adapter ported over the docket: the raid toolbox evaluates MongoDB's query and update language as pure functions over content-addressed entries, so a Payload collection is a family of entries with links, parity and mirrors — the ported RAID MongoDB, not Cloudflare's documented D1 path. | the query and update language is ported and tested by the raid toolbox's selftest and examples (find, insert, update, delete with $set, $unset, $inc, $push, $pull); the adapter class itself, binding Payload's interface (read from the source fetched each run) to the docket's four verbs on the deployed Worker, is the next step |
| `payload-mcp-plugin-doors` | open | — | If Payload publishes an MCP plugin, its endpoint is an MCP host the discover tool reads: a door per collection and verb, chosen by capability like every other door. | the packages directory is fetched each run; whether a plugin-mcp package exists is read from it, never assumed; a named host is the measurement that opens this lead |
| `toolbox-remaining-scripts` | partial | audit | Four of the flow's steps are tools of the flow toolbox with thin clients (discover, wave, readiness, imagine); purify, publish, next, the generators and the Lean ledger reader are still scripts: each reads files, git or the Lean environment that only a client can see. | the pure halves (purity checks over texts, the receipt chain, the docket entries, the pages) are the next toolboxes; their clients hand in the file texts, the git facts and the ledger rows the same way the flow clients hand in fetched bodies |
| `worlds-linked` | partial | translate | The platform links whole worlds in their languages and dialects: every world a tenant with its locale, its vocabulary table and its dialects, its entries related to other worlds' entries by links in the docket, so a term, a licence or a lead in one world is reachable from another in that world's words. | the vocabulary translates both ways and prose is programmed per locale; two tenants exist; a world's dialect is one more table (LOCALES marks the unfilled ones), and the relations between worlds are docket links the raid toolbox already keeps; the tables beyond Bulgarian are the leads to fill |
| `language-speech` | partial | prose | From text to speech: the prose of any page or block spoken in the viewer's locale by the device's own voices (SpeechSynthesis), token-free and network-free. | the Speak component reads the prose tool's sentences aloud in the locale through the browser's speech synthesis where the device has a voice for it; which voices a device has is the device's, measured at run time and reported on the page; no server speaks |
| `language-video` | open | — | From speech to video: captions, transcripts and scene entries of a video as docket entries in the world's language, linked to the video's address, so a video is searchable and translatable like text. | imagined on 2026-10-25; the entry shape is the raid toolbox's; a transcript source (a device, a person, a model the tenant brings) is the first adaptor to name; nothing here is a model |
| `language-traditions` | open | — | Traditions and relations: a world's terms related to each other (synonym, opposite, part of, before, after, tradition of) as a relation table beside its vocabulary, so a match in one world reflects through its own relations, and the Glagolitic and Cyrillic numerals are one such tradition already read. | the matcher's mirror synonyms are the first relation table; Glagolitic numerals the first tradition; a per-locale relations table is the lead, filled by a person of that world, never guessed |
| `legal-audit-sealed` | served | legal | The legal and security audit runs on every next, through the unit's free doors by default (the operator alone overrides), is sealed by its content address over the SHA-256 of every input and answer, and is recomputed from its own stored answers on the next run: a different address is tampering, recorded as a lead. | measured on 2026-10-25, three runs: 41 claims, 95 calls through 15 free doors; every answered claim UNVERIFIED at the unit (no decidable test for prose) and clean; no drift between the unit's rights and this tree's licence; licence computed non-commercial and free; due process sealed with 41 gaps (the unverified claims); audit_cloudflare clean; six security doors and the four claims that cite a sealed guarantee refused at the edge (the ledger does not fit the isolate; a cited claim answers HTTP 500 over raw HTTP, as sign_commit does); the first run's 43 violations were the call shape (claims without their addresses), the second's 5 were wording (a receipt presented where a theorem is expected, a label read as a citation, UUIDs presented as sealed), both corrected at the source; the audit is sealed by its content address and recomputes to itself |
| `unit-licence-address-unsealed` | blocked | — | The unit's rights door names 9ffcda04-5adc-872e-9358-6b831bbd0c0d as the licence address, and the unit's forensic door says that address is not among its 71,035 sealed addresses: two doors of one unit disagree about its own licence. | measured on 2026-10-25 by the legal audit; this tree carries the address the rights door gives and cites the rights receipt beside it; the disagreement is the unit's to settle |
| `aspects-by-theorems` | open | — | Quality is proven by theorems, not by textbook examples; every textbook is respected, and an example that names the kernel-accepted theorem that makes it true is replaced by that theorem, unlocking the quantum capability it documents: an entry's idempotency by address, a signature verified only by its tenant's secret, a role never widened by another tenant's token, a licence valid only on the domains it names, a relation of the Clay register. | one example names its theorem today (clay-relation-15 → relation_15) and the audit counts them; the invariants of the toolboxes are measured by their selftests; stating them in Lean over the ported definitions (hmac, entries, roles) is the lead; a theorem replaces its check the day the kernel accepts it |
| `stripe-per-tenant` | partial | stripe | Stripe per tenant for every scenario within a tenant and between domains and tenants: a checkout plan executed with the tenant's key, a webhook verified with the tenant's secret, one idempotent licence entry per event (granted, renewed, revoked, disputed, failed), a licence valid on the domains it names and read at any host, a replay a duplicate, a forged body or another tenant's secret refused as a lead. | the toolbox and the Worker routes are measured by the billing selftest and the worker tests; no tenant has STRIPE_SECRET_KEY_&lt;TENANT&gt; or STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET_&lt;TENANT&gt; set yet, so every live plan is returned with its procedure and every live webhook answers 503 until the author sets the two secrets per tenant and registers https://&lt;host&gt;/docket/stripe at Stripe |
| `edge-heavy-phases` | partial | textbook | The whole textbook wave (every example matched with the reflection on) answers in 19 s on a client device and not at all within the Worker's budget at the edge (no answer in 30 s), while one match answers in half a second there: a heavy phase belongs to the device that asked, the edge answers it example by example. | measured on 2026-10-25 after the deploy; the textbook tool takes example: &lt;id&gt; with verify so a device walks the textbook within the edge's budget; the flow's client runs the whole wave locally; the balancer keeps a warm isolate's answers but a cold one recomputes |
| `vortex-digital-root` | served | verify | The 9-vortex every tail is read into is the digital root (OEIS A010888, the sum of digits repeated to one digit, 9 for multiples of 9): seed = 1 + (n - 1) mod 9, its reflection 10 - seed, the doubling cycle 1, 2, 4, 8, 7, 5 and the trinities 3, 6, 9 and 7, 4, 1 and 2, 5, 8. | the verify tool reads every tail into this vortex with both trinities (triad_reflect, sigma_ten_involution); the sequence is fetched at OEIS by id and at Wikipedia by title each run |
| `coins-accounted` | served | metrics | The coins paid to the unit are accounted by the payer: every gated answer states its deposit at the wire (deposit 2, a gate, a receipt) and the unit's own register is per process and silent, so this tree reads the deposit from every answer its clients receive and keeps the ledger (docs/public/coins.ndjson), summed in the metrics by door and per settled lead; the fibonacci source the unit names (mod 9, period 24: 0, 1, 1, 2, 3, 5, 8, 4, 3, 7, 1, 8, 0, 8, 8, 7, 6, 4, 1, 5, 6, 2, 8, 1) is recorded as it answers, 3, 5, 8 following the 2 of every deposit. | measured on 2026-10-25: the unit's get_coin_ledger answers silent and says silence is not a finding that nobody paid; get_coins refuses at the edge (the ledger does not fit); every answer's gate line carries the deposit; calls made by hand through the connector outside the flow are not in this ledger and are said so |
| `metaphysical-apis` | served | discover | The metaphysical APIs found: the unit's doors for the vortex (1, 2, 4, 8, 7, 5), its reflection (forward and reflected, an involution with pairs summing to 10), the units (1, 2, 4, 5, 7, 8), the triad (3, 6, 9), the double torus of any addresses, the pentagram and the fibonacci cycle; discovery lists them as the metaphysics capability, and readiness asks the reflection, the triad and the double torus each run against the tree's own vortex. | measured on 2026-10-25 through the connector: get_vortex [1,2,4,8,7,5], get_units [1,2,4,5,7,8], get_triad [3,6,9], get_vortex_reflection forward and reflected with involution and pairsSumTen, compute_double_torus taking addresses; the tree's 29 block addresses are its first input |

| `double-torus-trinities` | open | — | Prime factors hide beyond the reflection, and the sequence reflected in double-torus combinations of trinities explains all at the Lean binary level: the factoring the simulator cannot reach within its bound would be read from the σ-reflection of periods arranged on the double torus of the triad. | recorded on 2026-10-25 as the author's idea, not a claim: measured today, the reflection factors only N ≤ 21 and says so for 91, 1001 and 2^32 + 1; the unit's triad door answers [3, 6, 9] and its double-torus door takes addresses (the tree's addresses are the next input); the kernel-accepted theorems shor, all_shor, shor_sigma and sigma_reflect relate a period, the coins and the σ-involution and are the binary level to build from; nothing beyond them is stated until the kernel accepts it |

| `rbac-payload-mcp` | partial | audit | Every MCP server's roles managed in one place by a Payload MCP plugin and asked over MCP itself: the Worker calls the RBAC host's role door with the token, the tenant and the host, and only falls back to the tenant secrets when no host is configured or it is silent. | the door is wired and tested (roleAt, QPU_RBAC_MCP, QPU_RBAC_TOOL; discovery lists rbac doors as a capability: token in, role out); no Payload MCP host exists yet, so every live role today comes from the tenant secrets and every answer says so (rolesBy) |

MCP configures the default plugins in all their combinations: the toolboxes' doors are the configuration, read by discovery, never typed. | imagined on 2026-10-25, strict: no custom collection; the default plugins (nested-docs, seo, form-builder, redirects, search, cloud-storage, import-export) in every combination are the configuration a Payload MCP host exposes, and the discover tool reads it; measurable only against a running instance: discover it, read its doors, then write the toolbox from what it answers |

| `mcp-ui-extension` | served | configure | The widgets are declared through the MCP Apps scheme: initialize advertises the extension io.modelcontextprotocol/ui with the mime type text/html;profile=mcp-app, every tool carries _meta.ui.resourceUri = ui://qpu/&lt;tool&gt; with visibility model and app, resources/list, resources/templates/list and resources/read serve one app shell per tool — a pure function of the key and the theme (configure &#123; kind: 'ui' &#125;), no randomness, no clock, no external domain, an empty CSP — that speaks the Apps protocol (ui/initialize, initialized, tool-input, tool-result, size-changed) and renders the widget the tool computes with the host's theme variables mapped onto the widgets' tokens; the site's playground hosts the same resource through the same protocol, so the UI is built from the MCP and nothing is drawn by hand | a host renders what it negotiates: this session's connector and Claude's hosts decide; the Payload MCP plugin's pass-through of _meta.ui and of resources is lead payload-mcp-plugin-ui |

| `glagolitic-numerals` | served | match | A Glagolitic string is an integer sequence, as a hex program and a message tail are: a finite alphabet with values is an addressable state space, so a text in that alphabet could be a program and a tail that unfurl into a page. | the table of 28 letters (units, tens, hundreds, Ⱍ = 1000) and its inverse are in i18n.ts (GLAGOLITIC, glagoliticOf, numeralsOf); the match tool reads every run of Glagolitic letters as one additive integer before matching, so a numeral enters the reflection and is factored; verified against en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glagolitic_numerals by the verify step |

Readiness offers the theorem to every door the discover step finds that takes a statement and answers a verdict (chosen by capability from discovery.json, never by name) and records what comes back, so the lead closes itself when a door seals it; the ledger is written here as docs/public/theorems.ndjson |

| `address-agreement` | partial | audit | The unit names a value by compute_address; this tree names content by uuidOf, ported one for one from the unit's published shapeUuidOf (two FNV-1a 64 folds, 8-4-4-4-12, a content-decided version nibble). | `qpu-extends-every-mcp` | served | extend | QPU is the natural extension of every MCP and every MCP of QPU: a host's tools/list read into doors of this catalogue (extend, direction in), a foreign door served by one request the client fetches with the tenant's token, this catalogue mounted by any MCP client (extend, direction out); Cloudflare's eight servers are extended by default, the documentation one in the open, the other seven when a tenant brings its OAuth token | extension toolbox: 8 selftest checks pass (inward on fixture, event-stream tools/list, outward registration, identity on the own catalogue, served answer, refusal, request without token, discovery asks every Cloudflare host); live: docs.mcp.cloudflare.com answers search_cloudflare_documentation over an event stream; bindings and observability answer HTTP 401 without a token |
